w_{t,q}\bigl(P_{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}}(\cdot;t,q)\bigr)=Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\cdot;q,t),

where
{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}
are transposed Young diagrams
{\bm{\lambda}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}
, respectively. Hence, with the specialization
\bm{\beta}=(b_{0},b_{1},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1})
, as in (
C.3
), we have

Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\bm{\beta}=(b_{0},b_{1},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1});q,t)=P_{{\bm{\lambda}}^{\prime}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\prime}}(\bm{\alpha}=(b_{0},b_{1},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1});t,q).

The following claim states that we can encode the Macdonald ascending process
C.4
of Young diagrams as an
N
-particle non-intersecting
\theta
-Bernoulli random walk.

Claim C.3

(
[
24
, Corollary 3.11]
)
.

The transition probability of Definition
C.2
under the specializations (
C.3
) is non-degenerate only for partitions
{\bm{\lambda}},{\bm{\mu}}
with at most
n
parts, i.e.
{\bm{\lambda}}=(\lambda_{1}\geqslant\lambda_{2}\geqslant\dots\geqslant\lambda_{n})
and
{\bm{\mu}}=(\mu_{1}\geqslant\mu_{2}\geqslant\dots\geqslant\mu_{n})
. Further, if we set
t=q^{\theta}
and identify

{\bm{x}}=({\mathsf{x}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{2},\dots,{\mathsf{x}}_{N})\in\mathbb{W}_{\theta}^{N},\quad{\mathsf{x}}_{i}=\mu_{i}-\theta(i-1),\quad{\mathsf{x}}_{i}+e_{i}=\lambda_{i}-\theta(i-1),\quad 1\leqslant i\leqslant n,

(C.7)

then
e_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and the transition probability is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{{\mathsf{b}},q}(\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}+{\bm{e}}|\bm{{\mathsf{x}}})=\frac{1}{\Pi(\rho,\bm{\beta}=b_{\mathsf{t}})}\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}\frac{q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}+\theta e_{i}}-q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{j}+\theta e_{j}}}{q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}}-q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}}b_{\mathsf{t}}^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e_{i}}.

(C.8)

The following lemma gives the asymptotics of the Macdonald polynomial
P_{\bm{\lambda}}(1,t,\dots,t^{N-1};q,t)
.

Lemma C.4
.

Given a Young diagram
{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{Y}_{N}
, we identify it as an particle configuration

\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}=({\mathsf{x}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{2},\dots,{\mathsf{x}}_{N})\in\mathbb{W}_{\theta}^{N},\quad{\mathsf{x}}_{i}=\lambda_{i}-\theta(i-1)\quad 1\leqslant i\leqslant N,

(C.9)

then

\displaystyle P_{\bm{\lambda}}(1,t,\dots,t^{N-1};q,t)=q^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\theta({\mathsf{x}}_{i}+(i-1)\theta)}\prod_{i<j\leqslant N}\frac{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{y}}_{j})}{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}+\theta)}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(i\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(\theta)},

(C.10)

Given sequences a sequence of Young diagrams

\displaystyle\bm{\lambda}^{(N)}=(\lambda_{1}^{(N)}\geqslant\lambda_{2}^{(N)}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant\lambda_{N}^{(N)})\in\mathbb{Y}_{N},\quad N\geqslant 1,

such that

1.

There exists a constant
C>0
,
\lambda_{1}^{(N)}\leqslant CN

2.

There exists a
1
-Lipschitz nondecreasing function
h:{\mathbb{R}}\mapsto[0,\theta]
, and when
N\rightarrow\infty

\displaystyle\frac{\theta}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta\left(\frac{\lambda_{i}^{(N)}-(i-1)\theta}{N}\right)\rightarrow\partial_{x}h(x),

(C.11)

in distribution. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\ln P_{\bm{\lambda}}(1,t,\dots,t^{N-1};q,t)=\frac{1}{2\theta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ln(1-e^{\kappa|x-y|}){\rm d}h(x){\rm d}h(y)-\theta\int_{0}^{1}x\ln(1-e^{\kappa\theta x}){\rm d}x\\
-\kappa\theta^{2}\int\frac{(1-x)x}{e^{-\kappa\theta x}-1}{\rm d}x+\frac{\kappa}{\theta}\int xh(x){\rm d}h(x)+\frac{\kappa\theta^{2}}{3}+\oo(1).\end{split}

(C.12)

Proof.

We can reorganize (
C.6
), and rewrite it in terms of the
q
-Gamma functions

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\prod_{i<j\leqslant N}\frac{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(t^{j-i+1};q)_{\infty}}{(t^{j-i};q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i<j\leqslant N}\frac{(q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{y}}_{j}};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}+\theta};q)_{\infty}}\frac{(q^{\theta(j-i+1)};q)_{\infty}}{(q^{\theta(j-i)};q)_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i<j\leqslant N}\frac{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{y}}_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j})}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\theta(j-i))}{\Gamma_{q}(\theta(j-i+1))}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i<j\leqslant N}\frac{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{y}}_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j})}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\Gamma_{q}(\theta)}{\Gamma_{q}(i\theta)}.

The claim (
C.10
) follows.
For the asymptotics (
6.8
), simply write

\lambda_{i}^{(N)}-(i-1)\theta={\mathsf{x}}_{i}
. By our assumption (
C.11
), the exponent in the first term on the righthand side of (
C.10
) is

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{N^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\theta({\mathsf{x}}_{i}+(i-1)\theta)=\frac{\kappa}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\theta i}{N}\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}}{N}+\frac{\kappa\theta^{2}}{3}+\OO(1/N)=\frac{\kappa}{\theta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}xh(x){\rm d}h(x)+\frac{\kappa\theta^{2}}{3}+\oo(1).

(C.13)

We recall the following estimates for the
q
-Gamma function for
q\leqslant 1
, the first statement follows from
[
49
, Corollary]
, and the second follows from

[
49
, Theorem 1]
.

\displaystyle\left|\ln\Gamma_{q}(z)-(z-1/2)\ln\left(\frac{q^{z}-1}{q-1}\right)-\frac{1}{\ln q}\int_{-\ln q}^{-z\ln q}\frac{u{\rm d}u}{e^{u}-1}\right|\leqslant C,\quad z\geqslant\theta

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\ln\Gamma_{q}(z)-\ln\left(\frac{q^{z}-1}{q-1}\right)\right|\leqslant\frac{C}{z},\quad z\geqslant\theta.

So we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\ln\Gamma_{q}(\theta)-\ln\Gamma_{q}(i\theta)=-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(i\theta\ln\left(\frac{q^{i\theta}-1}{q-1}\right)+\frac{1}{\ln q}\int_{0}^{-i\theta\ln q}\frac{u{\rm d}u}{e^{u}-1}\right)+\OO(1/N)\\
&=\frac{\theta}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\kappa}{N}\right)-\theta\int_{0}^{1}x\ln(1-e^{\kappa\theta x}){\rm d}x-\kappa\theta^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{(1-x)x}{e^{-\kappa\theta x}-1}{\rm d}x+\OO(\ln N/N).\end{split}

(C.14)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\ln\frac{\Gamma({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}+\theta)}{\Gamma({\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j})}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\theta\ln\left(\frac{q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}-1}{q-1}\right)+\OO\left(\sum_{i<j}\frac{1}{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i<j}\theta\ln\left(\frac{q^{{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}}-1}{q-1}\right)+\OO\left(\ln N/N\right)\\
&=-\frac{\theta}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\kappa}{N}\right)+\frac{1}{2\theta}\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ln(1-e^{\kappa|x-y|}){\rm d}h(x){\rm d}h(y)+\OO(\ln N/N).\end{split}

(C.15)

where we used that
{\mathsf{x}}_{i}-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}\geqslant\theta(i-j)
for the second inequality; in the last equality we used
Lemma

2.7
. The claim (
C.12
) follows from plugging (
C.13
), (
C.14
) and (
C.15
) into (
C.10
).
∎

C.3
Proof of
Theorem

1.8

Thanks to the relation (
C.5
), the large deviation asymptotics of the skew Macdonald polynomials can be obtained from the large deviation principle of the dynamics (
C.8
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\ln Q_{{\bm{\lambda}}/{\bm{\mu}}}(\bm{\beta}=(b_{0},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1});q,t)=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\ln\left(\mathbb{P}({\bm{\lambda}}({\mathsf{T}})={\bm{\lambda}}|{\bm{\lambda}}(0)={\bm{\mu}})\Pi(\rho,\bm{\beta}=(b_{0},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1}))\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2\theta}\left.\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ln(1-e^{\kappa|x-y|}){\rm d}h(x,t){\rm d}h(y,t)+2\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}}xh(x,t){\rm d}h(x,t)\right)\right|_{t=T}^{t=0}+\oo(1),\end{split}

(C.16)

where we used
Lemma

C.4
.
The dynamics (
C.8
) and the non-intersecting
\theta
-Bernoulli random walks (
1.8
) differ by the drift
E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})D(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})
with

\displaystyle D(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}}):=\prod_{0\leq{\mathsf{t}}\leq\mathsf{T-1}}2^{N}b_{\mathsf{t}}^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}e_{i}(\mathsf{t})},

\displaystyle E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}}):=\prod_{0\leq{\mathsf{t}}\leq\mathsf{T}}\prod_{i<j}\frac{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-({\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{j}(\mathsf{t}))}}\frac{q^{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-q^{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}{q^{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-q^{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}},

\displaystyle\Pi((\rho,\bm{\beta}=(b_{0},\cdots,b_{{\mathsf{T}}-1})){\rm d}\mathbb{P}^{{\mathsf{b}},q}=E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})D(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}}){\rm d}\mathbb{P}.

Theorem

1.8
follows from plugging
Lemma

6.6
and the following lemma to (
C.16
). We remark that all the terms involving
\kappa
cancels out perfectly.

Lemma C.5
.

Adopt the notations and assumptions of
Theorem

1.8
, the following holds

\frac{1}{N^{2}}\ln E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left.\left(\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ln\frac{1-e^{\kappa|x-y|}}{-\kappa|x-y|}{\rm d}h(y,t){\rm d}h(x,t)+2\kappa\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}xh(x,t){\rm d}h(x,t)\right)\right|_{t=0}^{t=T}+\oo(1).

Proof.

We observe that

\displaystyle\frac{q^{x}-q^{y}}{x-y}=\frac{1}{\ln q}\int_{0}^{1}q^{\alpha x+(1-\alpha)y}{\rm d}\alpha,

so that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-({\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{j}(\mathsf{t}))}}\frac{q^{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-q^{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})+\theta e_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}{q^{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})}-q^{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}\\
&=\frac{q^{\theta e_{j}({\mathsf{t}})}\int_{0}^{1}q^{\alpha({\mathsf{x}}_{i}({\mathsf{t}})+\theta e_{i}({\mathsf{t}})-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}({\mathsf{t}})-\theta e_{j}({\mathsf{t}}))}q{\rm d}\alpha}{\int_{0}^{1}q^{\alpha({\mathsf{x}}_{i}({\mathsf{t}})-{\mathsf{x}}_{j}({\mathsf{t}}))}{\rm d}\alpha}=q^{\theta e_{j}({\mathsf{t}})}\mathbb{E}_{q^{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})-{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}}}[q^{\alpha\theta(e_{i}(\mathsf{t})-e_{j}(\mathsf{(}t))}],\end{split}

where for a real number
\beta
,
\mathbb{E}_{\beta}
denotes the expectation over the variable
\alpha\in[0,1]
given, for any bounded measurable function
f
on
[0,1]
, by

\mathbb{E}_{\beta}[f(\alpha)]=\frac{\int_{0}^{1}f(\alpha)\beta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\alpha}{\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{\alpha}{\rm d}\alpha}\,.

We recall that
q=e^{\kappa/N}
, and denote
t={\mathsf{t}}/N
, so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q^{{\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})-{\mathsf{x}_{j}(\mathsf{t})}}}}[q^{\alpha\theta(e_{i}(\mathsf{t})-e_{j}(\mathsf{(}t))}]=e^{\frac{\theta\kappa(e_{i}(\mathsf{t})-e_{j}(\mathsf{t}))}{N}\mathbb{E}_{e^{\kappa({x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)})}}[\alpha]+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\right)},

where
\OO(1/N^{2})
is uniform over the
{x}_{i}(t),{x}_{i}(t)
and over
\theta
in a bounded set. Recall that
e_{i}(\mathsf{t})=\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t}+1)-\mathsf{x}_{i}(\mathsf{t})
.
Hence, we deduce that if
f(y):=\mathbb{E}_{e^{y}}[\alpha]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})&=\prod_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}-1}e^{\frac{\kappa}{N}\sum_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N}\theta(i-1)e_{i}({\mathsf{t}})}\times\\
&\times e^{\theta\kappa\sum_{0\leq{\mathsf{t}}\leq\mathsf{T}-1:t={\mathsf{t}}/N}\sum_{i<j}f(\kappa({{x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)}}))(({{x}_{i}(t+1/N)-{{x}_{j}(t+1/N)}})-({{x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)}}))+\OO(N)}\,.\end{split}

(C.17)

For the first term on the righthand side of (
C.17
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\kappa}{N^{3}}\sum_{{\mathsf{t}}=0}^{{\mathsf{T}}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\theta({\mathsf{x}}_{i}({\mathsf{t}}+1)-{\mathsf{x}}_{i}({\mathsf{t}}))=\frac{\kappa}{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(i-1)\theta(x_{i}(T)-x_{i}(0))\\
&=\frac{\kappa}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\theta i}{N}(x_{i}(T)-x_{i}(0))+\OO(1/N)=\left.\frac{\kappa}{\theta}\int_{\mathbb{R}}xh(x,t){\rm d}h(x,t)\right|_{t=0}^{t=T}+\oo(1).\end{split}

(C.18)

For the second term on the righthand side of (
C.17
), let

\displaystyle F(x)=\int_{0}^{\kappa x}f(y){\rm d}y=\int_{0}^{\kappa x}\partial_{y}\ln\int_{0}^{1}e^{\alpha y}{\rm d}\alpha{\rm d}y=\ln\int_{0}^{1}e^{\alpha\kappa x}{\rm d}\alpha=\ln\frac{1-e^{\kappa x}}{-\kappa x},

so that
F^{\prime}(x)=\kappa f(\kappa x)
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\kappa f(\kappa({{x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)}}))({{x}_{i}(t+1/N)-{{x}_{j}(t+1/N)}}-({{x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)}}))\\
&=F({{x}_{i}(t+1/N)-{{x}_{j}(t+1/N)}})-F({{x}_{i}(t)-{{x}_{j}(t)}})+\OO\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\right).\end{split}

where the error is again uniform since
f
is uniformly Lipschitz as

|\partial_{y}f(y)|\leq 2\,.

Therefore, for the second term on the righthand side of (
C.17
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{\theta}{N^{2}}\sum_{i<j}F({{x}_{i}(T)-{{x}_{j}(T)}})-F({{x}_{i}(0)-{{x}_{j}(0)}})+\oo(1)\\
&=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left.\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}F(|x-y|)\rho(y;{\bm{x}}(t))\rho(x;{\bm{x}}(t)){\rm d}y{\rm d}x\right|_{t=0}^{t=T}+\OO(1/N),\end{split}

(C.19)

where we finally used that
F
is uniformly Lipschitz on compacts.
Finally, we can see that the above right hand side is a continuous function of
H(y;{\bm{x}}(T))
and
H(y;{\bm{x}}(0))
equipped with the uniform topology, provided its derivative is a probability measure. The proof follows the ideas from
Lemma
2.7
, so we omit the details. It follows from plugging (
C.18
) and (
C.19
) into (
C.17
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\ln E(\{\bm{{\mathsf{x}}}({\mathsf{t}})\}_{0\leqslant{\mathsf{t}}\leqslant{\mathsf{T}}})=\frac{1}{2\theta}\left.\left(\iint_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ln\frac{1-e^{\kappa|x-y|}}{-\kappa|x-y|}{\rm d}h(y,t){\rm d}h(x,t)+2\kappa\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}xh(x,t){\rm d}h(x,t)\right)\right|_{t=0}^{t=T}+\oo(1).\end{split}

∎
